A large portion of Cloudflare's workforce -- from engineering to sales and everything in between -- uses Cloudflare OS every day to help them do their jobs.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ca target=\\\"_blank\\\" rel=\\\"noopener noreferrer\\\" href=\\\"/cloudflare/cloudflare-os/blob/main/docs/images/q3-planning-workspace.png\\\"\\u003e\\u003cimg src=\\\"/cloudflare/cloudflare-os/raw/main/docs/images/q3-planning-workspace.png\\\" alt=\\\"A Q3 planning workspace in Cloudflare OS, with an AI-generated slide deck\\\" style=\\\"max-width: 100%;\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThis is not a traditional computer operating system. We use the term \\\"operating system\\\" in two senses:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cul dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eAn operating system for \\u003cem\\u003ethe company\\u003c/em\\u003e to be productive with AI, in a way that is safe, so that the security team can sleep at night.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eAn operating system for AI workloads, analogous to the sense in which a traditional operating system manages compute workloads.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ul\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eCloudflare OS provides three things in particular:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003col dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eAn agent chat UI where you can ask agents to do tasks, preloaded with knowledge about how your company operates.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eSandboxed application development, so that you can ask agents to build \\\"gadgets\\\" (small personal apps) and safely share what you've built with others.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eA security framework, called Gatekeepers, that applies guardrails to both agents and apps such that non-technical users can safely \\\"go nuts\\\" and nothing bad will happen.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ol\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWe are making Cloudflare OS open source so that others can copy it and customize it for their own company. The idea is not that your company uses Cloudflare OS, but rather that you make it \\\"\\u003cem\\u003eYour Company\\u003c/em\\u003e OS\\\".\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch2 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eQuick Start\\u003c/h2\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-quick-start\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003einstall pnpm\\u003c/a\\u003e, then do:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\\\" I've made my first move. Your turn.\\\"\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\\"Make an issue dashboard for this GitHub repo.\\\" (Attach a repo; requires that the GitHub integration is configured.)\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\\"Fix the typos in this Google Doc.\\\" (Attach a doc; requires that the Google integration is configured.)\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ul\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWARNING: Early access\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-warning-early-access\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: WARNING: Early access\\\" href=\\\"#warning-early-access\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eCloudflare OS is in a state of heavy development. This repository is actually version 2, a complete rewrite taking what we learned from version 1 and putting it on a new foundation.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eAs of the August 2026 release, Cloudflare OS v2 is very capable, but still has many rough edges. We know, and we're working on it. For now, consider this an \\\"early access\\\" release.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch2 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eOverview: What is Cloudflare OS really?\\u003c/h2\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-overview-what-is-cloudflare-os-really\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Overview: What is Cloudflare OS really?\\\" href=\\\"#overview-what-is-cloudflare-os-really\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eGadgets: A new way of thinking about software\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-gadgets-a-new-way-of-thinking-about-software\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Gadgets: A new way of thinking about software\\\" href=\\\"#gadgets-a-new-way-of-thinking-about-software\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eCloudflare OS is more than just another chatbox with connectors. The system revolves around a new approach to software, where every user runs their own copy of the productivity apps they use.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWhen you create a slide deck in Cloudflare OS, you are not calling out to some SaaS software running in the cloud. The system creates a \\u003cem\\u003eprivate instance\\u003c/em\\u003e of the slide deck software \\u003cem\\u003ejust for you\\u003c/em\\u003e. We call this a \\\"gadget\\\". This instance runs in a separate sandbox from everyone else's slide decks.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThis has two profound effects:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003col dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eIt's impossible for the slide deck app to have a security bug that leaks your slides to an attacker. The Cloudflare OS sandbox controls all access to your private instance of the app.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eIf you want, you can freely modify the code. If the slide deck app is missing a feature you need, you can just ask your agent to add it. And because of point 1, it's totally safe to do so.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ol\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThis is a big departure from the last 25 years of cloud architecture and \\\"Software as a Service\\\", but we think AI has changed the equation. When any user is capable of prompting an agent to add the features they need, the centralized model of software stops making sense.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eGatekeepers: A capability-based security layer\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-gatekeepers-a-capability-based-security-layer\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Gatekeepers: A capability-based security layer\\\" href=\\\"#gatekeepers-a-capability-based-security-layer\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eGatekeepers are like supercharged MCP servers.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWhen you introduce an agent or Gadget to an external resource, a Gatekeeper is created to manage that access. The Gatekeeper is a piece of software specific to each external service which moderates a Gadget's connection to that service. It:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cul dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eProvides a clean Cap'n Web API to the service (wrapping whatever API the service provides natively).\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eHandles authorization (e.g. via OAuth).\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eEnforces narrow access to only the specific resource the user intended.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eLogs every action the Gadget (or agent) performs, for your review.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eFor any action which has side effects, provides the human user an opportunity to approve or deny the action (\\\"human in the loop\\\").\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ul\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eOn the last point, Gatekeepers implement a significant advancement in the state of the art. Traditionally, human-in-the-loop setups require the human to approve actions \\u003cem\\u003esynchronously\\u003c/em\\u003e. When the agent wants to do something, it has to \\u003cem\\u003estop\\u003c/em\\u003e and wait for said approval before it can continue. This is annoying: you give your agent a task, then walk away and get a coffee, only to come back and find the agent got stuck on an approval on the first step and has made no progress. As a result, people often give in and set their agents to \\\"auto-approve\\\", or \\u003ccode\\u003e--dangerously-skip-permissions\\u003c/code\\u003e, which is, obviously, unsafe.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eGatekeepers provide a better way: When the agent (or Gadget) performs an action that requires approval, the Gatekeeper will \\u003cem\\u003esimulate\\u003c/em\\u003e the outcome locally, allowing the agent to proceed and queue up more actions. The Gatekeeper tells the agent that the action completed, and if the agent tries to read back the results, the Gatekeeper gives it simulated results. Once the agent is done, the user may approve or reject the actions in bulk, or one-by-one, but either way, they can do it later, when it is convenient.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eLogistically, each Gatekeeper is implemented as a separate Worker. In the future, we envision Gatekeeper services being deployed and maintained independently from OS instances, but the details have yet to be worked out. For now, we have provided a few interesting Gatekeepers in this repository which you can deploy together with your own OS instance.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThink of an office suite\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-think-of-an-office-suite\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Think of an office suite\\\" href=\\\"#think-of-an-office-suite\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThe basic user experience of Cloudflare OS is something like an online office suite, like Google Docs or MS Office. But, imagine that instead of a fixed set of file types (document, spreadsheet, slide deck), each file -- or \\\"Gadget\\\" -- is potentially its own custom application, written by AI to serve exactly your needs.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eJust like office docs, each gadget is private by default, but can be shared -- securely -- in order to collaborate with your team or your friends.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eJust like office docs, you can have thousands of them. You can create them on a whim.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eJust like office docs, you can start from \\\"templates\\\" -- called \\\"Blueprints\\\". But where an office template is just some content, a Blueprint specifies a whole application.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eLike office docs, you can create new templates (blueprints) from your own docs (Gadgets) and share them with others. But when you do so, you are sharing the code for a whole app.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eIt kind of is an Operating System\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-it-kind-of-is-an-operating-system\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: It kind of is an Operating System\\\" href=\\\"#it-kind-of-is-an-operating-system\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThe OS terminology isn't \\u003cem\\u003eentirely\\u003c/em\\u003e marketing. Cloudflare OS is actually analogous to an operating system on a technical level.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cmarkdown-accessiblity-table\\u003e\\u003ctable\\u003e\\n\\u003cthead\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003cth\\u003eNormal OS\\u003c/th\\u003e\\n\\u003cth\\u003eCloudflare OS\\u003c/th\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003c/thead\\u003e\\n\\u003ctbody\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003ekernel\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003epackages/workshop-backend\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003edevice drivers\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003epackages/gatekeeper-*\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eshell\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003epackages/workshop-frontend\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eprocesses\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003egadgets\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eexecutables\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eblueprints\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eusers\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eusers\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eACLs\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eshared permissions\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003e???\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eagents\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tbody\\u003e\\n\\u003c/table\\u003e\\u003c/markdown-accessiblity-table\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eOur \\\"kernel\\\" is in the workshop-backend package. The backend legitimately does a lot of things similar to real OS kernels: it connects users to programs and devices (Gadgets and Gatekeepers, as we call them) while implementing security by sandboxing applications and enforcing access control.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eIn this analogy, Gatekeepers -- which connect users and agents to external services -- are like drivers -- which connect users and programs to external devices.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThere is one thing that traditional OSes don't really manage today, but Cloudflare OS does: AI agents. If you think about it, this is really a missing feature in traditional OSes. We believe that AI agents cannot simply be treated as users. They must be accountable to a human user, while at the same time having their own restricted permissions. Agents do work by writing snippets of code and executing them on the fly. The ideal security model for all of this is capability-based security, not access control lists. See what I mean? Perhaps traditional OSes ought to give AI agents special treatment, too.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eBuilt on Workers, by the Workers team\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-built-on-workers-by-the-workers-team\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Built on Workers, by the Workers team\\\" href=\\\"#built-on-workers-by-the-workers-team\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eCloudflare OS is built on \\u003ca href=\\\"https://workers.cloudflare.com\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eCloudflare Workers\\u003c/a\\u003e, making heavy use of \\u003ca href=\\\"https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eDurable Objects\\u003c/a\\u003e, \\u003ca href=\\\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eDynamic Workers\\u003c/a\\u003e, and \\u003ca href=\\\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/durable-object-facets-dynamic-workers/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eFacets\\u003c/a\\u003e in particular. Every workspace is its own Durable Object, every Gadget runs in a Dynamic Worker Facet, and Gatekeepers also install facets into each workspace to manage access to remote services.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eCloudflare OS is, in fact, built by the very people who built Workers itself. It uses cutting-edge features of the Workers Runtime -- in fact, Dynamic Workers, Facets, and several other features were added to the runtime specifically to support Cloudflare OS, with more to come. Studying the Cloudflare OS source code is a great way to understand how the Workers Runtime team thinks Workers should be used.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eBeing built on Workers does not mean that Cloudflare OS can only run on Cloudflare. In fact, \\u003ca href=\\\"https://github.com/cloudflare/workerd\\\"\\u003e\\u003ccode\\u003eworkerd\\u003c/code\\u003e, the Cloudflare Workers Runtime, is itself open source\\u003c/a\\u003e, and Cloudflare OS can run entirely on top of it on your own servers.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch2 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThe Cloudflare OS coding agent is actually a fully multi-purpose agent that can perform arbitrary tasks; like other popular coding agents, you don't have to code with it. You can use it to build Gadgets, but you can also skip the Gadget and just have the agent perform tasks directly. The Cloudflare OS agent is a \\u003ca href=\\\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eCode Mode\\u003c/a\\u003e agent -- it performs tasks by writing and immediately executing snippets of code. It can be connected to external resources using Gatekeepers (like MCP -- see below).\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eBuild apps with AI\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-build-apps-with-ai\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Build apps with AI\\\" href=\\\"#build-apps-with-ai\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWhile you can code a Gadget by hand if you want, the expectation is that AI writes the code for you. Cloudflare OS features a built-in coding agent that will build whatever you ask it, test it for you, and debug errors.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eYou can choose your LLM. Cloudflare OS works with many major AI model providers and self-hosted models, with more providers being added all the time.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eBecause of the tightly-integrated and simplified nature of the platform, even when using the same underlying AI models, the Cloudflare OS coding agent often performs better and faster with fewer tokens than a general-purpose coding agent would.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eCollaborate with AI\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-collaborate-with-ai\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Collaborate with AI\\\" href=\\\"#collaborate-with-ai\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eEvery app built with Cloudflare OS automatically has an agent-friendly API. That means, after you've asked AI to build the app, you can also ask AI to collaborate with you \\u003cem\\u003einside\\u003c/em\\u003e the app. No need to build an MCP server nor integrate a custom agent loop. It's just there by default.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThis works because the client and server portions of a Gadget are required to communicate via \\u003ca href=\\\"https://github.com/cloudflare/capnweb\\\"\\u003eCap'n Web RPC\\u003c/a\\u003e. This is a win-win:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003col dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eCap'n Web is extremely low-boilerplate, which makes it easy for agents to work with. You basically just define a method on your server, then call it from your client, as if it were a local call.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eMeanwhile, it means that the server necessarily exposes an easy-to-understand API which could be called directly by an agent. The AI Agent harness uses \\u003ca href=\\\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eCode Mode\\u003c/a\\u003e for tool calling, making it trivial to expose the Gadget's API directly for the agent to invoke.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ol\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eReal-time Multiplayer\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-real-time-multiplayer\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Real-time Multiplayer\\\" href=\\\"#real-time-multiplayer\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eYou can share your Gadget just like you'd share a document in a typical online office suite. You can give specific users access, or create a share link that provides access to anyone who opens it. And just like those online office suites, you'll be able to see your collaborators' actions in real time.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThis works because every Gadget is backed by a \\u003ca href=\\\"https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eDurable Object\\u003c/a\\u003e, Cloudflare's stateful serverless primitive which makes real-time multiplayer collaboration easy. It's so easy that the coding agent just implements it by default, without being asked.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eBlueprints: Share your code\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-blueprints-share-your-code\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Blueprints: Share your code\\\" href=\\\"#blueprints-share-your-code\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eIf you've created a Gadget that might be useful to others, but you don't want to share the Gadget itself, you can instead share a Blueprint, allowing other people to create their own copy of the Gadget. A Blueprint is essentially a copy of the code.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eIt may sound simple, but Blueprints are a major change from cloud software tradition. Traditionally, if you create a web app that you want to share with other users, you host the app on your server, and the users connect to that. Blueprints are much more like mobile apps and traditional PC apps: every user runs their own copy of the software.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eIn the age of AI, this change is critically important. On one hand, AI empowers an individual developer to build more than ever, but it is still difficult for an individual developer to maintain an online service; this eliminates the need. On the other hand -- and even more importantly -- allowing each user to run their own copy of the software empowers the user to \\u003cem\\u003echange\\u003c/em\\u003e the software to meet their needs, using AI. No need to file a feature request, no need to beg the developer to prioritize it. The end user can solve their own problems.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eSandboxed and secure by default\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-sandboxed-and-secure-by-default\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Sandboxed and secure by default\\\" href=\\\"#sandboxed-and-secure-by-default\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eEach Gadget runs in a secure sandbox that prevents it from talking to the internet at all without your explicit consent. In particular:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cul dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eThe server runs in a \\u003ca href=\\\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eDynamic Worker\\u003c/a\\u003e which has had its access to the internet disabled. It can only communicate with specific external resources that you have explicitly designated, via \\u003ca href=\\\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-environment-live-object-bindings/\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\"\\u003eWorkers Bindings\\u003c/a\\u003e.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eThe client code runs in a sandboxed iframe. This iframe can communicate with its server only via a Cap'n Web RPC session provided over \\u003ccode\\u003epostMessage()\\u003c/code\\u003e to the parent frame. The iframe is otherwise blocked from accessing the internet (to the maximum extent allowed by browsers, via \\u003ccode\\u003eContent-Security-Policy\\u003c/code\\u003e and iframe sandbox settings).\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ul\\u003e\\n\\u003cdiv class=\\\"markdown-heading\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003ch3 tabindex=\\\"-1\\\" class=\\\"heading-element\\\" dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eCapability-based access control\\u003c/h3\\u003e\\u003ca id=\\\"user-content-capability-based-access-control\\\" class=\\\"anchor\\\" aria-label=\\\"Permalink: Capability-based access control\\\" href=\\\"#capability-based-access-control\\\"\\u003e\\u003csvg data-component=\\\"Octicon\\\" class=\\\"octicon octicon-link\\\" viewBox=\\\"0 0 16 16\\\" version=\\\"1.1\\\" width=\\\"16\\\" height=\\\"16\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\"\\u003e\\u003cpath d=\\\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eEach agent, and each Gadget, by default has access to nothing. Even if you've configured the Gadget Workshop with access to external accounts, agents and Gadgets do NOT automatically get to use them.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eInstead, you must \\u003cem\\u003eintroduce\\u003c/em\\u003e each agent (or Gadget) to any particular resources you want it to access. For instance, you may introduce a GitHub repository by pasting a link to it, or clicking \\\"add resource\\\" and selecting it via the UI. An agent can also request an introduction to a resource it thinks it needs, which you can then provide or deny.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThis differs from most agent harnesses, where MCP servers are configured upfront, making broad access to all your services ambiently available to the agent in every chat. contribution.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eAI has made writing code easy. The hard part, today, is not writing the code, but reviewing it, making sure quality stays high, and keeping the product coherent. In that light, unfortunately, external code contributions are \\\"donating\\\" the easy part of the job, while creating more of the hard work.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWith that said, we are happy to accept small, trivially-verified PRs that fix a problem. However, we ask that you refrain from submitting low-value PRs (e.g. typo fixes) or PRs that are more than a dozen or so lines. Such PRs will be closed with a reference to this guideline.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eIf you have a big idea you'd like us to consider, feel free to \\u003ca href=\\\"https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-os/discussions\\\"\\u003eopen a discussion\\u003c/a\\u003e about it.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eThis policy may change in the future as the project matures. DirectoryRichtextContent-module__SharedMarkdownContent__hHXUL\" data-hpc=\"true\" containertiming=\"hpc\"><article class=\"markdown-body entry-content container-lg\" itemprop=\"text\"><div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h1 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS: An AI productivity environment</h1><a id=\"user-content-cloudflare-os-an-ai-productivity-environment\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Cloudflare OS: An AI productivity environment\" href=\"#cloudflare-os-an-ai-productivity-environment\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS is an \"operating system\" for AI productivity originally developed for use inside Cloudflare. A large portion of Cloudflare's workforce -- from engineering to sales and everything in between -- uses Cloudflare OS every day to help them do their jobs.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\"><a target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\" href=\"/cloudflare/cloudflare-os/blob/main/docs/images/q3-planning-workspace.png\"><img src=\"/cloudflare/cloudflare-os/raw/main/docs/images/q3-planning-workspace.png\" alt=\"A Q3 planning workspace in Cloudflare OS, with an AI-generated slide deck\" style=\"max-width: 100%;\"></a></p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This is not a traditional computer operating system. We use the term \"operating system\" in two senses:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>An operating system for <em>the company</em> to be productive with AI, in a way that is safe, so that the security team can sleep at night.</li>\n<li>An operating system for AI workloads, analogous to the sense in which a traditional operating system manages compute workloads.</li>\n</ul>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS provides three things in particular:</p>\n<ol dir=\"auto\">\n<li>An agent chat UI where you can ask agents to do tasks, preloaded with knowledge about how your company operates.</li>\n<li>Sandboxed application development, so that you can ask agents to build \"gadgets\" (small personal apps) and safely share what you've built with others.</li>\n<li>A security framework, called Gatekeepers, that applies guardrails to both agents and apps such that non-technical users can safely \"go nuts\" and nothing bad will happen.</li>\n</ol>\n<p dir=\"auto\">We are making Cloudflare OS open source so that others can copy it and customize it for their own company. The idea is not that your company uses Cloudflare OS, but rather that you make it \"<em>Your Company</em> OS\".</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Quick Start</h2><a id=\"user-content-quick-start\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Quick Start\" href=\"#quick-start\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">To quickly run Cloudflare OS locally, <a href=\"https://pnpm.io/\" rel=\"nofollow\">install pnpm</a>, then do:</p>\n<div class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\"><pre class=\"notranslate\"><code>pnpm run-local\n</code></pre><div class=\"zeroclipboard-container\">\n    <clipboard-copy aria-label=\"Copy code to clipboard\" class=\"ClipboardButton btn btn-invisible js-clipboard-copy m-2 p-0 d-flex flex-justify-center d=\"M5 1.75C5 .784 5.784 0 6.75 0h7.5C15.216 0 16 .784 16 1.75v7.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 14.25 11h-7.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 5 9.25Zm1.75-.25a.25.25 0 0 0-.25.25v7.5c0 .138.112.25.25.25h7.5a.25.25 0 0 0 9.28a.751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018L6 10.94l6.72-6.72a.75.75 0 0 1 1.06 0Z\"></path>\n</svg>\n    </clipboard-copy>\n  </div></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Then visit: <a href=\"http://localhost:8787\" rel=\"nofollow\">http://localhost:8787</a></p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This runs the whole stack locally on wrangler and workerd. This is not meant for production use, but is a quick way to see what the product does.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Alternatively, you can <a href=\"https://os.cloudflare.app/deploy\" rel=\"nofollow\">deploy to your Cloudflare account</a>.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">(More options at the end of this readme.)</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">What to try</h3><a id=\"user-content-what-to-try\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: What to try\" href=\"#what-to-try\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Try prompts like:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>\"Make slides for my upcoming meeting with a customer.\" (This will use the built-in slides blueprint.)</li>\n<li>\"Make a collaborative whiteboard app.\" (This will create a new app from scratch.)</li>\n<li>\"Make a tic tac toe game.\" followed by \"I'll be X and you be O. I've made my first move. Your turn.\"</li>\n<li>\"Make an issue dashboard for this GitHub repo.\" (Attach a repo; requires that the GitHub integration is configured.)</li>\n<li>\"Fix the typos in this Google Doc.\" (Attach a doc; requires that the Google integration is configured.)</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">WARNING: Early access</h3><a id=\"user-content-warning-early-access\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: WARNING: Early access\" href=\"#warning-early-access\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS is in a state of heavy development. This repository is actually version 2, a complete rewrite taking what we learned from version 1 and putting it on a new foundation.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">As of the August 2026 release, Cloudflare OS v2 is very capable, but still has many rough edges. We know, and we're working on it. For now, consider this an \"early access\" release.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Overview: What is Cloudflare OS really?</h2><a id=\"user-content-overview-what-is-cloudflare-os-really\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Overview: What is Cloudflare OS really?\" href=\"#overview-what-is-cloudflare-os-really\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Gadgets: A new way of thinking about software</h3><a id=\"user-content-gadgets-a-new-way-of-thinking-about-software\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Gadgets: A new way of thinking about software\" href=\"#gadgets-a-new-way-of-thinking-about-software\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS is more than just another chatbox with connectors. The system revolves around a new approach to software, where every user runs their own copy of the productivity apps they use.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">When you create a slide deck in Cloudflare OS, you are not calling out to some SaaS software running in the cloud. The system creates a <em>private instance</em> of the slide deck software <em>just for you</em>. We call this a \"gadget\". This instance runs in a separate sandbox from everyone else's slide decks.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This has two profound effects:</p>\n<ol dir=\"auto\">\n<li>It's impossible for the slide deck app to have a security bug that leaks your slides to an attacker. The Cloudflare OS sandbox controls all access to your private instance of the app.</li>\n<li>If you want, you can freely modify the code. If the slide deck app is missing a feature you need, you can just ask your agent to add it. And because of point 1, it's totally safe to do so.</li>\n</ol>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This is a big departure from the last 25 years of cloud architecture and \"Software as a Service\", but we think AI has changed the equation. When any user is capable of prompting an agent to add the features they need, the centralized model of software stops making sense.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Gatekeepers: A capability-based security layer</h3><a id=\"user-content-gatekeepers-a-capability-based-security-layer\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Gatekeepers: A capability-based security layer\" href=\"#gatekeepers-a-capability-based-security-layer\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Gatekeepers are like supercharged MCP servers.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">When you introduce an agent or Gadget to an external resource, a Gatekeeper is created to manage that access. The Gatekeeper is a piece of software specific to each external service which moderates a Gadget's connection to that service. It:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Provides a clean Cap'n Web API to the service (wrapping whatever API the service provides natively).</li>\n<li>Handles authorization (e.g. via OAuth).</li>\n<li>Enforces narrow access to only the specific resource the user intended.</li>\n<li>Logs every action the Gadget (or agent) performs, for your review.</li>\n<li>For any action which has side effects, provides the human user an opportunity to approve or deny the action (\"human in the loop\").</li>\n</ul>\n<p dir=\"auto\">On the last point, Gatekeepers implement a significant advancement in the state of the art. Traditionally, human-in-the-loop setups require the human to approve actions <em>synchronously</em>. When the agent wants to do something, it has to <em>stop</em> and wait for said approval before it can continue. This is annoying: you give your agent a task, then walk away and get a coffee, only to come back and find the agent got stuck on an approval on the first step and has made no progress. As a result, people often give in and set their agents to \"auto-approve\", or <code>--dangerously-skip-permissions</code>, which is, obviously, unsafe.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Gatekeepers provide a better way: When the agent (or Gadget) performs an action that requires approval, the Gatekeeper will <em>simulate</em> the outcome locally, allowing the agent to proceed and queue up more actions. The Gatekeeper tells the agent that the action completed, and if the agent tries to read back the results, the Gatekeeper gives it simulated results. Once the agent is done, the user may approve or reject the actions in bulk, or one-by-one, but either way, they can do it later, when it is convenient.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Logistically, each Gatekeeper is implemented as a separate Worker. In the future, we envision Gatekeeper services being deployed and maintained independently from OS instances, but the details have yet to be worked out. For now, we have provided a few interesting Gatekeepers in this repository which you can deploy together with your own OS instance.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Think of an office suite</h3><a id=\"user-content-think-of-an-office-suite\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Think of an office suite\" href=\"#think-of-an-office-suite\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">The basic user experience of Cloudflare OS is something like an online office suite, like Google Docs or MS Office. But, imagine that instead of a fixed set of file types (document, spreadsheet, slide deck), each file -- or \"Gadget\" -- is potentially its own custom application, written by AI to serve exactly your needs.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Just like office docs, each gadget is private by default, but can be shared -- securely -- in order to collaborate with your team or your friends.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Just like office docs, you can have thousands of them. You can create them on a whim.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Just like office docs, you can start from \"templates\" -- called \"Blueprints\". But where an office template is just some content, a Blueprint specifies a whole application.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Like office docs, you can create new templates (blueprints) from your own docs (Gadgets) and share them with others. But when you do so, you are sharing the code for a whole app.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">It kind of is an Operating System</h3><a id=\"user-content-it-kind-of-is-an-operating-system\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: It kind of is an Operating System\" href=\"#it-kind-of-is-an-operating-system\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">The OS terminology isn't <em>entirely</em> marketing. Cloudflare OS is actually analogous to an operating system on a technical level.</p>\n<markdown-accessiblity-table data-catalyst=\"\"><table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th>Normal OS</th>\n<th>Cloudflare OS</th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>kernel</td>\n<td>packages/workshop-backend</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>device drivers</td>\n<td>packages/gatekeeper-*</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>shell</td>\n<td>packages/workshop-frontend</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>processes</td>\n<td>gadgets</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>executables</td>\n<td>blueprints</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>users</td>\n<td>users</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>ACLs</td>\n<td>shared permissions</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>???</td>\n<td>agents</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Our \"kernel\" is in the workshop-backend package. The backend legitimately does a lot of things similar to real OS kernels: it connects users to programs and devices (Gadgets and Gatekeepers, as we call them) while implementing security by sandboxing applications and enforcing access control.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">In this analogy, Gatekeepers -- which connect users and agents to external services -- are like drivers -- which connect users and programs to external devices.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">There is one thing that traditional OSes don't really manage today, but Cloudflare OS does: AI agents. If you think about it, this is really a missing feature in traditional OSes. We believe that AI agents cannot simply be treated as users. They must be accountable to a human user, while at the same time having their own restricted permissions. Agents do work by writing snippets of code and executing them on the fly. The ideal security model for all of this is capability-based security, not access control lists. See what I mean? Perhaps traditional OSes ought to give AI agents special treatment, too.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Built on Workers, by the Workers team</h3><a id=\"user-content-built-on-workers-by-the-workers-team\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Built on Workers, by the Workers team\" href=\"#built-on-workers-by-the-workers-team\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS is built on <a href=\"https://workers.cloudflare.com\" rel=\"nofollow\">Cloudflare Workers</a>, making heavy use of <a href=\"https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Durable Objects</a>, <a href=\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Dynamic Workers</a>, and <a href=\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/durable-object-facets-dynamic-workers/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Facets</a> in particular. Every workspace is its own Durable Object, every Gadget runs in a Dynamic Worker Facet, and Gatekeepers also install facets into each workspace to manage access to remote services.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS is, in fact, built by the very people who built Workers itself. It uses cutting-edge features of the Workers Runtime -- in fact, Dynamic Workers, Facets, and several other features were added to the runtime specifically to support Cloudflare OS, with more to come. Studying the Cloudflare OS source code is a great way to understand how the Workers Runtime team thinks Workers should be used.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Being built on Workers does not mean that Cloudflare OS can only run on Cloudflare. In fact, <a href=\"https://github.com/cloudflare/workerd\"><code>workerd</code>, the Cloudflare Workers Runtime, is itself open source</a>, and Cloudflare OS can run entirely on top of it on your own servers.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Features</h2><a id=\"user-content-features\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Features\" href=\"#features\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">General multi-purpose agent</h3><a id=\"user-content-general-multi-purpose-agent\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: General multi-purpose agent\" href=\"#general-multi-purpose-agent\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">The Cloudflare OS coding agent is actually a fully multi-purpose agent that can perform arbitrary tasks; like other popular coding agents, you don't have to code with it. You can use it to build Gadgets, but you can also skip the Gadget and just have the agent perform tasks directly. The Cloudflare OS agent is a <a href=\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Code Mode</a> agent -- it performs tasks by writing and immediately executing snippets of code. It can be connected to external resources using Gatekeepers (like MCP -- see below).</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Build apps with AI</h3><a id=\"user-content-build-apps-with-ai\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Build apps with AI\" href=\"#build-apps-with-ai\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">While you can code a Gadget by hand if you want, the expectation is that AI writes the code for you. Cloudflare OS features a built-in coding agent that will build whatever you ask it, test it for you, and debug errors.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">You can choose your LLM. Cloudflare OS works with many major AI model providers and self-hosted models, with more providers being added all the time.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Because of the tightly-integrated and simplified nature of the platform, even when using the same underlying AI models, the Cloudflare OS coding agent often performs better and faster with fewer tokens than a general-purpose coding agent would.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Collaborate with AI</h3><a id=\"user-content-collaborate-with-ai\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Collaborate with AI\" href=\"#collaborate-with-ai\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Every app built with Cloudflare OS automatically has an agent-friendly API. That means, after you've asked AI to build the app, you can also ask AI to collaborate with you <em>inside</em> the app. No need to build an MCP server nor integrate a custom agent loop. It's just there by default.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This works because the client and server portions of a Gadget are required to communicate via <a href=\"https://github.com/cloudflare/capnweb\">Cap'n Web RPC</a>. This is a win-win:</p>\n<ol dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Cap'n Web is extremely low-boilerplate, which makes it easy for agents to work with. You basically just define a method on your server, then call it from your client, as if it were a local call.</li>\n<li>Meanwhile, it means that the server necessarily exposes an easy-to-understand API which could be called directly by an agent. The AI Agent harness uses <a href=\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Code Mode</a> for tool calling, making it trivial to expose the Gadget's API directly for the agent to invoke.</li>\n</ol>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Real-time Multiplayer</h3><a id=\"user-content-real-time-multiplayer\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Real-time Multiplayer\" href=\"#real-time-multiplayer\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">You can share your Gadget just like you'd share a document in a typical online office suite. You can give specific users access, or create a share link that provides access to anyone who opens it. And just like those online office suites, you'll be able to see your collaborators' actions in real time.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This works because every Gadget is backed by a <a href=\"https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Durable Object</a>, Cloudflare's stateful serverless primitive which makes real-time multiplayer collaboration easy. It's so easy that the coding agent just implements it by default, without being asked.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Blueprints: Share your code</h3><a id=\"user-content-blueprints-share-your-code\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Blueprints: Share your code\" href=\"#blueprints-share-your-code\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">If you've created a Gadget that might be useful to others, but you don't want to share the Gadget itself, you can instead share a Blueprint, allowing other people to create their own copy of the Gadget. A Blueprint is essentially a copy of the code.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">It may sound simple, but Blueprints are a major change from cloud software tradition. Traditionally, if you create a web app that you want to share with other users, you host the app on your server, and the users connect to that. Blueprints are much more like mobile apps and traditional PC apps: every user runs their own copy of the software.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">In the age of AI, this change is critically important. On one hand, AI empowers an individual developer to build more than ever, but it is still difficult for an individual developer to maintain an online service; this eliminates the need. On the other hand -- and even more importantly -- allowing each user to run their own copy of the software empowers the user to <em>change</em> the software to meet their needs, using AI. No need to file a feature request, no need to beg the developer to prioritize it. The end user can solve their own problems.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Sandboxed and secure by default</h3><a id=\"user-content-sandboxed-and-secure-by-default\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Sandboxed and secure by default\" href=\"#sandboxed-and-secure-by-default\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Each Gadget runs in a secure sandbox that prevents it from talking to the internet at all without your explicit consent. In particular:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>The server runs in a <a href=\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Dynamic Worker</a> which has had its access to the internet disabled. It can only communicate with specific external resources that you have explicitly designated, via <a href=\"https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-environment-live-object-bindings/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Workers Bindings</a>.</li>\n<li>The client code runs in a sandboxed iframe. This iframe can communicate with its server only via a Cap'n Web RPC session provided over <code>postMessage()</code> to the parent frame. The iframe is otherwise blocked from accessing the internet (to the maximum extent allowed by browsers, via <code>Content-Security-Policy</code> and iframe sandbox settings).</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Capability-based access control</h3><a id=\"user-content-capability-based-access-control\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Capability-based access control\" href=\"#capability-based-access-control\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Each agent, and each Gadget, by default has access to nothing. Even if you've configured the Gadget Workshop with access to external accounts, agents and Gadgets do NOT automatically get to use them.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Instead, you must <em>introduce</em> each agent (or Gadget) to any particular resources you want it to access. For instance, you may introduce a GitHub repository by pasting a link to it, or clicking \"add resource\" and selecting it via the UI. An agent can also request an introduction to a resource it thinks it needs, which you can then provide or deny.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This differs from most agent harnesses, where MCP servers are configured upfront, making broad access to all your services ambiently available to the agent in every chat. Capability-based introductions keep each agent restricted to only the access it actually needs for the job at hand.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Get Started</h2><a id=\"user-content-get-started\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Get Started\" href=\"#get-started\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 This is not the right way to run the OS on a production server, but it works fine for trying it out on your local machine.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Your data will be stored in a subdirectory named <code>.wrangler</code>.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Deploy to your own server using <code>workerd</code></h3><a id=\"user-content-deploy-to-your-own-server-using-workerd\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Deploy to your own server using workerd\" href=\"#deploy-to-your-own-server-using-workerd\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\"><strong>COMING SOON</strong></p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS can run entirely on <code>workerd</code>, Cloudflare's open source runtime for Workers. In fact, the \"run locally\" instructions above use <code>workerd</code> under the hood. We are still working on documentation and tooling to help you smoothly deploy the OS on top of <code>workerd</code> on your own servers. If you are feeling adventurous, <a href=\"https://github.com/cloudflare/workerd/blob/main/src/workerd/server/workerd.capnp\">read the low-level documentation for workerd config</a> (or point your agent at it) and have a go.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h4 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Configuring external services</h4><a id=\"user-content-configuring-external-services\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Configuring external services\" href=\"#configuring-external-services\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Many Gatekeepers require configuration in order to be able to connect to third-party services, including obtaining OAuth client credentials for each service. Unfortunately, many service providers intentionally do not make this easy, since the intended audience for OAuth is developers.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Each gatekeeper package contains instructions for API</a></li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Developing</h2><a id=\"user-content-developing\" class=\"anchor\" class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Contributing</h3><a id=\"user-content-contributing\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Contributing\" href=\"#contributing\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">At this time, we are not seeking outside contribution.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">AI has made writing code easy. The hard part, today, is not writing the code, but reviewing it, making sure quality stays high, and keeping the product coherent. In that light, unfortunately, external code contributions are \"donating\" the easy part of the job, while creating more of the hard work.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">With that said, we are happy to accept small, trivially-verified PRs that fix a problem. However, we ask that you refrain from submitting low-value PRs (e.g. typo fixes) or PRs that are more than a dozen or so lines. Such PRs will be closed with a reference to this guideline.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">If you have a big idea you'd like us to consider, feel free to <a href=\"https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflare-os/discussions\">open a discussion</a> about it.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This policy may change in the future as the project matures. Until then, thank you for your understanding.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Credits</h2><a id=\"user-content-credits\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Credits\" href=\"#credits\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Cloudflare OS has far too many open source dependencies to list here. But, we'd like to highlight a few that do particularly heavy lifting:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li><a href=\"https://pi.dev/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Pi</a> (specifically, <code>pi-agent-core</code>), which made it easy to support every LLM provider with one API.</li>\n<li><a href=\"https://microsoft.github.io/monaco-editor/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Monaco</a> which makes it too easy to embed a beautiful text editor -- for those of us who still look at the code.</li>\n<li><a href=\"https://yjs.dev/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Yjs</a>, which we use extensively to sync code changes between clients and agents and replay histories.</li>\n<li><a href=\"https://vite.dev/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Vite</a>, which makes the development loop so pleasant.</li>\n</ul>\n</article></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div
